## Milestone 3 — intake and planning closeout

**READY_FOR_REVIEW for the locally usable, labeled demo workflow.** The owner's
attached Milestone 3 request authorized direct implementation and isolated checks;
no other implementation agent or standalone Codex launcher was started. The allowed
unavailable-model path is complete. A real bounded local request did not yield a
validated result; this is an explicit live-planning limitation, tracked as WB-15,
not successful research. Milestone 4 has not begun.

### Implemented and source-verified

- Work has approachable issue/feature actions and recent requests; `/intake` has
  saved requests and a small paginated list. Original required title/description
  and optional literal Lua error commit before asynchronous work. Stable UUIDs
  reconcile lost submission responses; original input is immutable. Supplemental
  clarification stays separate, durable and visible after restart.
- A separate worker claims durable planning jobs using short SQLite transactions,
  token-bound 90-second leases and at most three attempts. Context is bounded and
  saved before inference. Provider I/O runs outside the writer transaction. Progress
  is actual queued/investigating/completed/waiting-for-information/failed state.
  Failed planning retains input and presents explicit retry; no paid fallback.
- Structured proposals retain experience/value, feasibility, uncertainty, evidence,
  scope/exclusions, acceptance, risks/dependencies, stages and visual classification.
  Actual inspected evidence has relative labels, hashes and sanitized excerpts.
  Product requirements do not establish addon behavior or a root cause. Issues
  produce an investigation task or a specific in-app clarification, never a presumed
  implemented fix or unsupported duplicate assertion.
- Local provider reuses the existing tool-free HTTP/key/coordination pattern without
  importing pilot code. Fixed loopback only; redirects refused, no proxy forwarding,
  24,000-byte input/output limits, 2,600 output tokens, 30-second HTTP timeout,
  validated schema and returned model identity on success. No model installation,
  service restart, Codex/Claude invocation, routing settings or paid activation.
- Manual revision-bound attachments use private application SQLite storage: PNG
  mockups/in-game-context references and text evidence. Type/size/CRC/dimensions/
  bounded PNG decompression are validated; no upload paths or active content are
  served. Visual or unknown implementation impact requires both image roles.
  Research-only scope requires an explicit new revision and its own review.
  No automatic mockup generator, actual game capture or simulator evidence is added.
- Approve / required-explanation changes / optional-note decline bind to the exact
  proposal revision and artifact set. Immutable receipts save before worker
  application. Saved/pending, applied, stale and rejected semantics stay separate.
  Concurrent decisions serialize; old revision/artifact bindings cannot approve a
  newer proposal. Approval changes no existing fixture/pilot hold, execution policy,
  attempt, verification, publication state or provider permission.
- Applied approval enqueues an idempotent decomposition job. Task graph and completion
  commit together; lease recovery, retries and response loss cannot duplicate it.
  Unique task keys, full approved-scope coverage, valid parent links and acyclic
  dependency edges are checked before task writes. Failed decomposition keeps the
  approval applied and offers retry without another approval of that revision.
- Existing Backlog projects the new planning records with acceptance, provenance,
  model and exact approved proposal identity. Parent links remain separate from
  dependencies. “Open in Backlog” focuses and expands the request so its child work
  is immediately visible; it no longer uses a text filter that hid the child.
  Local records are labeled separately from the surrounding simulated examples.
- Deterministic priority keeps serious independently verified bugs above ordinary
  features, gives unconfirmed reports timely triage, considers age and dependencies,
  and preserves manual overrides. New intake never manufactures verified severity.
  Severity, priority and execution eligibility are displayed separately. There is
  no execution adapter for these generated tasks.

Migration 0003 extends the existing application database/backlog. Models and typed
OpenAPI contracts are generated/checked with repository commands. No live migration
was performed. Ownership and all new transition owners are in `ownership.md`;
operator configuration and limits are in README. Accepted walnut/espresso/bronze/
ivory styling, original logo and workshop artwork remain; issue/feature artwork
appears selectively on the forms. WB-01–WB-05 were not reopened.

### WB-15 diagnosis, budget and exact observed result

Approach **1** was the preserved original Milestone 3 smoke: a 30-second urllib
request, 2,600 output-token cap, tool-free JSON-schema feature planning, persisted
request, generic failure after 30.05s. Exact original HTTP body/context and exception
were not retained, so they cannot be reconstructed as exact evidence. The original
private temporary DB was already disposed. Its request intent and fixed transport
fields are retained in the smoke source and old artifact. No prior second inference
or hidden retry was found in the session/evidence.

Before another attempt, bounded read-only journal inspection established these
correlated server events for the sole slot:

| UTC event | Observed metadata |
| --- | --- |
| 22:32:31.186564 | Server selected a slot just after original intake/job creation |
| 22:32:32.707344 | Task 665350 began processing |
| 22:32:37.935020 | 2,215 prompt tokens processed in 5.23s |
| 22:32:41–22:33:00 | Generation advanced from 100 to 614 tokens at ~28 tokens/s |
| 22:33:01.211584 | Workbench retained its failed result at 30.05s |
| 22:33:02.190013 | Server canceled task 665350, then released it |

This establishes a client cutoff during active generation, rather than evidence
of lock contention, provider rejection or JSON/schema validation. The original
exception class remains unknown. At 28 tokens/s the allowed 2,600-token response
plus prefill can exceed 90 seconds, providing a measured reason for a planning-only
longer deadline. Existing health returned OK; `/v1/models`, `/props` and allowed
configuration fields agreed on **pilot-qwen38-27b**, model file
**Qwen3.8-27B-UD-IQ4_XS.gguf**, one slot, 32,768 context. Existing cycle/model locks
were free at inspection. The model service PID was unchanged; no restart, reload,
replacement model, stress workload or maintenance-policy write occurred.

Approach **2** was the distinct bounded correction:

- Planning-specific **absolute deadline**, default/cap 120s, operator environment
  bounded to 15–120s. API/browser timeouts were not raised. GET model discovery
  retains a 3s timeout. A socket monitor interrupts header waits/reads on deadline
  or worker shutdown. Actual socket-pair tests verify prompt cancellation.
- Worker passes its stop event; recovery lease is 150s, longer than the maximum
  inference deadline. No shared-record writer transaction spans inference. Existing
  maintenance/model locks still control admission; contention fails explicitly.
- Configured identity is observed from the existing fixed local service, and actual
  response identity must match. Durable safe diagnostic categories distinguish
  timeout, cancellation, contention, transport, HTTP rejection, incomplete output,
  invalid JSON, identity/configuration problems and structured validation. No raw
  provider error or credential enters API diagnostics. Redirect refusal remains.
- One guarded real feature-planning invocation in a private disposable database.
  A durable `wb15-second-approach.json` guard prevents another closeout run.
  **Both substantive approaches are now used; no further inference was performed.**

Actual result (`artifacts/m3-closeout/observed-smoke.json`):

| Property | Observed result |
| --- | --- |
| Intake before processing | API committed original request; queued state and no proposal verified before worker processing |
| Model | Configured and returned **pilot-qwen38-27b** |
| Timing / sizes | **50.574s**, input packet 9,894 bytes, response envelope 6,241 bytes |
| Validation | Real JSON/envelope, structured schema and allowed evidence IDs passed |
| Persistence | Research proposal revision **1**, ID `d26fcea1-f4b3-425a-9e41-6ee9f6574c72` |
| Intake state | `waiting_for_information`: model returned a specific clarification alongside the proposal |
| Permissions | No approval, detailed tasks, implementation or publication permission created |
| Exact material | Private request body without auth headers, raw response and DB under `.local/m3-closeout`; SHA-256 fingerprints in the safe smoke result |

Evidence scope was separately inspected. Selection remains bounded, now includes
relevant test directories, uses stable relevance ordering, tries at most twelve
ranked source candidates for up to two usable addon references, and retains file
hashes, excerpt line windows and observed checkout HEAD. Actual supplied addon
material was **docs/navigation.md** and **tests/fixtures/nav_fixture.lua**, at HEAD
`675dee0a29195afa8f28095d529273d0a88e2564`, plus two product documents. The input did
**not** contain the actual selected-step assertions or selection implementation.
The model has no file tools and was explicitly told to discuss supplied excerpts
only. Product-only context is visibly preliminary; missing addon evidence is an
explicit requirement before confirming feasibility. No general research framework
or broader filesystem/network access was introduced.

The content audit is deliberately narrower than transport success. The response
correctly cites the navigation documentation's named test files, identifies missing
contents/coverage, and asks before further inspection. It also calls Navigation
“nonvisual, noninteractive”; the supplied document describes a compact frame, arrow
and map drawings. **Nonvisual is contradicted and noninteractive is not established.**
The proposal and response are preserved unchanged as unverified model text; see
`observed-claim-audit.json`. No actual selected-step coverage or addon feasibility
was established. **Observed transport/schema/proposal persistence is verified;
fully grounded research acceptance and real local decomposition remain unaccepted.**
The existing WB-15 row carries the remaining gap and exhausted budget; demo tests

## WB-16 bounded safety repair (Milestone 3 pending acceptance)

This section supersedes the prior closeout's readiness and retained-review process
identity. The confirmed bypass was an authority error: `proposal_view` trusted the
planner's `visual=nonvisual`, removing both required image checks without a separate
human requirement. Binding that output to a revision preserved the wrong decision
boundary. No additional inference was needed to establish or repair this defect.

### Corrected authority and approval boundary

`intake_scopes` records append-only human visual scope and review stage, separately
from immutable original input and model proposals. Intake defaults to **Not sure**;
the two explicit alternatives are **Changes the in-game interface** and **Does not
change the interface**. Review exposes the same choice, a stage and revision reason.
No keywords or generated classification set this authority. Human scope edits append
a new scope revision and a new reviewable proposal without calling a provider.

The shared `visual_scope.py` domain computes requirements, review binding and
validation for API submission, worker decision application and task decomposition.
Implementation requires **both PNG proposed mockup and PNG in-game-context reference**
unless human scope is explicitly nonvisual and the proposal is also nonvisual.
Human visual/unclassified scope survives model nonvisual output and subsequent
proposal revisions. Model visual/uncertain impact strengthens human nonvisual
review requirements. Conflicts remain visible; none silently remove a safeguard.

Decisions store their scope revision and effective required artifact roles, as well
as the exact proposal/artifact binding and full immutable snapshot. Submission and
worker application reject stale or incomplete approval. Decomposition validates
again before calling an adapter and before committing tasks. A scope/proposal edit
during planning discards the obsolete result without overwriting the newer review.
The browser pins its open review and requires an explicit action to open a newer
revision; polling cannot silently switch the approval target.

Research requires an **explicit human research revision** and explicit exclusion
of all implementation, execution permission and publication. It can be approved
without implementation images while preserving the underlying interface obligation.
Changing the human stage to implementation does not relabel the old research
proposal; a later implementation proposal must be reviewed with the preserved
requirements. Generated work remains held under the existing execution/publication
boundary in every workflow, including valid explicit nonvisual work.

### Migration, audit preservation and remaining affected records

Migration **0004** gives all records lacking human authority scope revision 0,
**unclassified / legacy**, irrespective of historical model nonvisual claims.
Explicit human confirmation creates a new review. Existing original input, model
content, artifact bytes, decisions, snapshots, jobs and tasks remain intact. Applied
receipts keep their historical **applied** status; a separate scope-review marker
removes their current authority. Affected intake/task views show scope review and
held execution. Pending decisions revalidate on application; obsolete decomposition
fails before invoking a planner. Later relevant artifact changes are revalidated
in receipt/task projections and worker processing. No records are silently deleted.
Identical saved-command retries preserve idempotency and do not recreate authority.

Only disposable test databases and a copy of the saved isolated review were
migrated. **No live or pilot database was migrated or changed.** A byte-for-byte

## Milestone 4 — Models and execution policy closeout

Owner authorization: attached Milestone 4 request, executed directly in this chat,
without another implementation agent or standalone launcher. Delivery is local
application implementation and isolated verification. No provider inference,
paid activation, addon execution, live migration, account/authentication change,
commit, push, publication, maintenance/service restart or network/driver change.
WB-15 remains deferred at 2/2; WB-16 and prior accepted workflows remain intact.

### Configuration and ownership

`/models` is discoverable from navigation and Settings, with the accepted palette,
transparent emblem, existing workshop artwork and responsive controls. Preferred
backend, ordered permitted fallback, per-backend actual model ID, model-specific
effort, explicit substitution mode, separate fallback conditions and wait/block
behavior persist in append-only `execution_policies` (migration 0005). All providers'
activation values are constrained false. Defaults prohibit substitution, include
Local + Codex preference and leave Claude disabled. To express local-first permitted
fallback, select only explicitly configured fallback, order Codex next and include
Claude only deliberately. Saving preferences never changes an active attempt.

Settings use existing exact Host/Origin/CSRF/body limits, revision conflicts and
lost-response reconciliation. Drafts survive navigation/refresh; conflict reload is
explicit. Browser checks exposed a reload race; fields now lock during reload and
failed reload retains edits. Raw credential fields are not accepted, and ordinary
model inputs reject common credential prefixes. No secret or account-login editor.

The new configuration belongs to future Workbench-controlled execution. No
responsible execution worker currently consumes it, so acknowledged revision is
null and effective activation is never claimed. Existing planning configuration
remains separate. Pilot policy/reservations/receipts stay with their original
owner and the existing read-only adapter; there is no competing pilot writer.

### Installed discovery and capability limits

Inspected `codex --version` / `exec --help`, `claude --version` / `--help`, local
`llama-server --version`, allowlisted Codex cached catalog/model configuration and
relevant local planning/pilot escalation/settings/source contracts. No auth files,
provider account pages/private endpoints, receipts, raw invocation logs or runtime
DB copies. No inference or local smoke.

Observed: Codex CLI 0.166.0; Claude Code 2.1.316; llama.cpp 0.6.0-dev, build 11638,
commit 23deec025 (sanitized report retains 0.6.0). Codex cache had eight listed
models; configured default gpt-6.1-sol / high. That model's cached effort values
were low/medium/high/xhigh/max/ultra. These are observations, not hard-coded product
model versions or account entitlement. `discover_models.py` generates bounded,
allowlisted metadata; API only reads the server-selected sanitized report. Source
and timestamp remain visible. Catalog TTL is 24h; routing observation TTL is 120s.

Local identity/model-specific reasoning and Claude per-model effort are not
verified by installed help. Their forms accept an explicit model identifier labeled
not verified and offer unspecified effort only. Codex choices follow the selected
fresh model entry. Stale/unknown support cannot become routing eligibility. WB-15
specifically limits grounded planning and actual local decomposition; other local
capabilities remain unknown rather than declared failed.

### Routing and accounting

Pure evaluation visits at most three explicitly ordered candidates. It separates
purpose/capability, model support, observation freshness, availability, capacity,
verification failure and bounded retry inputs. Provider selection is separate from
activation and permission: a suitable candidate can be identified while activation
and execution remain held. Neither a choice nor a saved policy releases holds.
No same-model substitution, automatic unlimited retry, allowance-consumption target,
AI-selected routing or new capacity reservation exists.

GET preview uses saved preferences or visibly synthetic fallback, exhausted capacity,
disabled Claude, stale availability AND paid capacity, unknown observations, failed
verification and attempt-limit cases. Explanations include policy revision, model/
effort, purpose, reasons, constraints, sources and freshness. The preview cannot
execute, write policy/accounting, reserve allowance or change permissions.

Each newly claimed existing planning-job lease gets a unique `model_attempts` row
linked to the job and intake/task. Requested/reported identities, effort (unknown
when unreported), purpose, reason, policy revision if consumed, claim/end times,
outcome, verification association and sanitized token observation fields are retained.
Existing planner attempts have null policy revision because the new policy is not
consumed, plus a fixed recorded explanation of the existing adapter path. Start is
job claim, not inferred generation start. Expired leases retain uncertain outcome
without fabricated end; late results cannot replace a recovered attempt. Reported
model mismatch stays failed and retains requested versus actual identity.

Counts use distinct tasks across attempts; provider subtotals are not summed as
unique tasks. Demo/pilot/unknown sources are separate. No pilot attempt import is
implemented, preventing inferred attribution and double counting. Legacy counters
minus newly recorded leases remain unattributed; no historical rows, identities or
timestamps are manufactured. Structured completion is not independent verification.
Charts show only recorded backend/model/purpose counts, dated job outcomes and
per-response observed tokens when present. Recent details/usage are bounded to 100;
distribution to 500 groups and UTC dated outcomes to 120 day/outcome groups.

Provider account allowance/reset/windows and outside activity are unavailable.
Local response token counts can be retained with source/unit/time, but never turned
into subscription percentages. No estimated-consumption percentage, daily allowance
bucket, automatic API billing or overage path. Allocation maximum, personal reserve,
actual reset windows and pacing remain separate deferred concerns. Existing planning
safeguards (three job attempts, serial worker, coordinated local lock) are displayed
as those safeguards, not a hard quota/cost cap. See WB-17–WB-19.

### UX and unchanged semantics

`/models` is now an overview: compact saved preferred/fallback policy, compact
provider cards, allowance limitation and observed/demo activity. Forms live at
`/models/configure`, with direct per-provider links and one provider's model controls
shown at a time. Global routing settings and routing preview are secondary
expansions there. Settings links directly to configuration. The selected Models
navigation state covers the configuration route. Local quality copy is simply
“Planning quality needs verification”; WB-15 detail remains expandable. Unknown
availability and repetitive empty per-provider data stay in details; material stale
catalog/availability and unavailable executor evidence remain visible.

Drafts, revision conflicts, reload recovery and existing saves remain intact. A
visible unsaved note follows back/forward navigation; browser unload warns while
session storage retains edits. Catalog-backed model selectors replace always-visible
manual IDs, now under Advanced. Use installed default copies only an observed
configured preference; it is not runtime executor-default behavior. Unspecified
effort leaves the request unset, never claims actual effort. Stale historical effort
remains displayed as a disabled not-verified choice, so it can be explicitly cleared
to Unspecified. It is not silently changed or offered as verified support. Initial
loading now says Reading activity instead of prematurely claiming unavailability.

Inspection found a naming/presentation ambiguity, not a routing-policy semantic
defect: persisted `substitution=deny` also stops provider fallback; the other enum
allows only configured provider fallback. The control is now Provider fallback:
Keep preferred provider / Try configured fallback providers. Copy explains that a
saved order is unused in the former mode. Within-provider model replacement remains
prohibited and a different reported model remains a failed attempt. No enum/default,
routing evaluator, activation, permission, retry, requested/actual accounting or
existing planner execution behavior changed. Hashes confirm unchanged policy,
planning worker/provider and database-definition files against M4 closeout.

## Verification lifecycle timestamp capture correction (2026-11-04)

The fixture worker initialized finished_at before checking admission, capturing
started_at and invoking the verifier. This explains the historical 749us
inversion; these were incorrectly ordered event captures from the same UTC
clock, not differently sourced observations. created_at is captured when
building the saved request; started_at immediately before verifier invocation;
finished_at now after terminal handling (including stale/blocked/failure paths)
and immediately before the result update. Timestamps are event observations
committed atomically, not separate commit times. The API and UI already pass/map
these fields directly and need no changes. No clamping, sorting or audit repair.

Only verification.py and focused regression tests changed, plus this record.
Deterministic clock/adapter tests cover pass, failed checks and adapter error,
assert capture before/after verifier execution and exact persisted/API fields.
Stale/blocked checks retain null start and recorded finish. Focused: 9 passed.

including the user's review/history/completed result and original timestamps.

the original discrepancy remains visible as historical evidence. No migration,
provider activation, publication, new review instance, reset or worker restart.
The existing long-running worker will load the corrected code at its next
approved normal restart; no process was stopped to apply this correction.
